   551	    /// drift, clients switch on slugs that either no longer exist or were never documented. Checked
   552	    /// in both directions deliberately: an undocumented slug is as bad as a phantom one.
   553	    #[test]
   554	    fn spec_documents_every_reason_and_invents_none() {
   555	        use crate::reason::Reason;
   556	        use std::collections::HashSet;
   557	
   558	        let spec = std::fs::read_to_string(concat!(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"), "/docs/SPEC.md"))
   559	            .expect("read SPEC.md");
   560	        let section = spec
   561	            .split("### 10.1")
   562	            .nth(1)
   563	            .expect("SPEC.md must have a §10.1 reason section");
   564	        let section = section.split("\n## ").next().unwrap_or(section);
   565	
   566	        // Column 2 is the slug, in backticks. The header cell reads "Reason" without backticks and
   567	        // the separator row has none either, so both drop out here.
   568	        let mut documented: HashSet<String> = section
   569	            .lines()
   570	            .filter(|line| line.starts_with('|'))
   571	            .filter_map(|line| line.split('|').nth(2))
   572	            .map(str::trim)
   573	            .filter_map(|cell| cell.strip_prefix('`')?.strip_suffix('`'))
   574	            .map(ToString::to_string)
   575	            .collect();
   576	
   577	        // SPEC.md is frozen (ADR-0057); reasons added since are documented in OpenSpec specs.

   579	        if openspec_dir.exists() {
   580	            fn collect_reasons(dir: &std::path::Path, out: &mut HashSet<String>) {
   581	                if let Ok(entries) = std::fs::read_dir(dir) {
   582	                    for entry in entries.flatten() {
   583	                        let path = entry.path();
   584	                        if path.is_dir() {
   585	                            collect_reasons(&path, out);
   586	                        } else if path.extension().is_some_and(|ext| ext == "md") {
   587	                            if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(&path) {
   588	                                for r in Reason::ALL {
   589	                                    let slug = r.as_slug();
   590	                                    if content.contains(&format!("`{slug}`")) {
   591	                                        out.insert(slug.to_string());
   592	                                    }
   593	                                }
   594	                            }
   595	                        }
   596	                    }
   597	                }
   598	            }
   599	            collect_reasons(openspec_dir, &mut documented);
   600	        }
   601	
   602	        let declared: HashSet<&str> = Reason::ALL.iter().map(|r| r.as_slug()).collect();
   603	        let documented_refs: HashSet<&str> = documented.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect();
   604	
   605	        let mut undocumented: Vec<_> = declared.difference(&documented_refs).collect();
   606	        undocumented.sort_unstable();
   607	        assert!(
   608	            undocumented.is_empty(),
   609	            "reasons missing from SPEC §10.1 or OpenSpec: {undocumented:?}"
   610	        );
   611	
   612	        let mut phantom: Vec<_> = documented_refs.difference(&declared).collect();
   613	        phantom.sort_unstable();
   614	        assert!(
   615	            phantom.is_empty(),
   616	            "SPEC §10.1 documents reasons that do not exist: {phantom:?}"
   617	        );
   618	    }

   661	Inter is the only bundled font. It is embedded through `typst-kit` plus `InterVariable.ttf`, which is
   662	also parsed with `ttf-parser` for text measurement (ADR-0049). The fonts directory is
   663	`LABELER_FONTS_DIR` (default `fonts/`); a custom fonts dir must contain `InterVariable.ttf`, and it

   670	## 10. Error model
   671	
   672	All errors return JSON:
   673	
   674	```json
   675	{ "error": { "code": "TemplateNotFound", "message": "…", "details": { "template": "xyz" } } }
   676	```
   677	
   678	| Code | Status | When |
   679	| --- | --- | --- |
   680	| `TemplateNotFound` | 404 | Unknown template id. |
   681	| `InvalidRequest` | 400 | Malformed JSON, bad path/param, out-of-range `start_slot`. |
   682	| `UnsupportedMediaType` | 415 | Missing/incorrect `Content-Type`. |
   683	| `InvalidOptionValue` | 422 | Option selection not allowed by the template. |
   684	| `MissingField` | 422 | A referenced `data` field is absent. |
   685	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | 422 | Layout item cannot be rendered (e.g. bad size/qr param). |
   686	| `TemplateInvalid` | 422 | Template fails structural validation (e.g. a dynamic `format.width` missing one bound). |
   687	| `UnsupportedFormat` | 422 | Endpoint/format mismatch or unknown unit. |
   688	| `BatchInvalid` | 422 | One or more `/batch` labels failed render-validation; `details.failures` lists them. |
   689	| `BatchTooLarge` | 413 | A `/batch` request exceeds the label cap (500). Note: `/batch` (and other JSON endpoints) can also return 413 with code `PayloadTooLarge` if the raw body exceeds the server's global limit. |
   690	| `PayloadTooLarge` | 413 | Request body exceeds the configured limit. On `POST /print` the limit is 64 KiB; on other JSON endpoints it is the server's global default (~2 MiB). Applies API-wide to any endpoint that reads a JSON body. |
   691	| `MediaMismatch` | 409 | Template `media_width` and the printer's loaded media width differ by more than 1 mm. Print is rejected by the preflight gate. |
   692	| `NotFound` | 404 | Unknown `/api/*` route (the API fallback). |
   693	| `RenderFailed` | 500 | Typst compile/encode failure. |
   694	| `SettingNotFound` | 404 | Unknown application setting key. |
   695	
   696	`code` strings are part of the contract; keep them stable. Authentication adds `Unauthorized` (401)
   697	and `Forbidden` (403); see §11.
   698	
   699	### 10.1 `details.reason`
   700	
   701	Decision: [ADR-0052](adr/0052-error-reason-discriminator.md). For the codes listed below, `details`
   702	is present and carries a stable `reason` slug naming the specific cause. `reason` is machine-readable
   703	and part of the contract; the `message` beside it is prose and is not — do not match on it.
   704	
   705	This is **not** a global promise. The four codes below are the complete set that carries a `reason`;
   706	every other code is unchanged, and `details` remains optional in the schema. Extending the set to a
   707	fifth code is a decision to record against ADR-0052, not a detail. A test asserts that this table and
   708	the `Reason` enum list exactly the same slugs, in both directions.
   709	
   710	| Code | Reason | When |
   711	| --- | --- | --- |
   712	| `TemplateInvalid` | `template_parse_failed` | The YAML did not parse. |
   713	| `TemplateInvalid` | `template_validation_failed` | The template parsed but failed structural validation. |
   714	| `TemplateInvalid` | `template_duplicate_id` | Two templates on disk declare the same id. |
   715	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `coord_out_of_frame` | A resolved coordinate falls outside the frame. |
   716	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `item_out_of_frame` | A resolved item box falls outside the frame. |
   717	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `line_endpoint_out_of_frame` | A resolved line endpoint falls outside the frame. |
   718	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `line_degenerate` | A line's start and end resolve to the same point. |
   719	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `edge_rect_inverted` | `to` is not above and to the right of `at`. |
   720	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `size_invalid` | A `size` component is not greater than 0. |
   721	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `size_auto_without_max` | `size` is `auto` with no matching `max_*` to resolve it. |
   722	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `size_auto_no_room` | `size` is `auto`, resolves via the anchor fallback (§4), and that fallback leaves no room (`<= 0`) on the axis; no binding `max_*` is at fault. |
   723	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `max_size_invalid` | The binding `max_*` component is not greater than 0. |
   724	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `image_source_missing` | An image item sets neither `src` nor `name`. |
   725	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `image_format_unsupported` | The image's MIME type or file extension is not supported. |
   726	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `image_data_invalid` | Inline image data is not a usable base64 `data:` URI. |
   727	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `image_asset_missing` | The referenced asset file does not exist. |
   728	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `image_asset_unreadable` | The asset file exists but could not be read. |
   729	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `image_asset_path_escapes` | The asset path resolves outside the assets directory. |
   730	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `assets_dir_unavailable` | The server's assets directory could not be resolved. |
   731	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `qr_error_correction_invalid` | `error_correction` is not one of L, M, Q, H. |
   732	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `dimension_exceeds_limit` | A resolved label dimension exceeds `max_label_dimension_mm` or is non-positive. |
   733	| `UnsupportedLayoutItem` | `container_padding_no_room` | A container's padding meets or exceeds its dimensions, leaving no room for content. |
   734	| `InvalidRequest` | `json_malformed` | The request body is not parseable JSON. `details.error` carries the parser's message. |
   735	| `InvalidRequest` | `request_body_invalid` | The request body could not be read. |
   736	| `InvalidRequest` | `path_param_invalid` | A path parameter could not be deserialized. |
   737	| `InvalidRequest` | `start_slot_out_of_range` | `start_slot` exceeds the sheet's slot count. |
   738	| `InvalidRequest` | `start_slot_not_applicable` | `start_slot` was sent for a non-sheet template; omit it. |
   739	| `InvalidRequest` | `options_not_supported` | An `option` selection was sent for a template that declares none. |
   740	| `InvalidRequest` | `batch_empty` | The batch contains no labels. |
   741	| `InvalidRequest` | `format_unknown` | `format` is not `png` or `pdf`. |
   742	| `InvalidRequest` | `format_not_applicable` | `format` was sent with `mode=print`; omit it. |
   743	| `InvalidRequest` | `interpolation_syntax` | An interpolation template has an unterminated `{` or an unmatched `}`. |
   744	| `InvalidRequest` | `template_id_invalid` | A template id is empty or contains characters other than letters, digits, `-` and `_`. |
   745	| `InvalidRequest` | `template_id_mismatch` | The template id in the body disagrees with the one in the path. |
   746	| `InvalidRequest` | `printer_id_invalid` | A printer id is empty or contains disallowed characters. |
   747	| `InvalidRequest` | `printer_id_mismatch` | The printer id in the body disagrees with the one in the path. |
   748	| `InvalidRequest` | `variable_key_invalid` | A variable key is empty or contains disallowed characters. |
   749	| `InvalidRequest` | `setting_value_invalid` | A setting's value failed its type or range rule. |
   750	| `InvalidRequest` | `datetime_pattern_invalid` | The datetime format pattern is not valid. |
   751	| `InvalidRequest` | `connector_unknown` | The request names a connector that does not exist. |
   752	| `InvalidRequest` | `connection_connector_missing` | A stored connection references a connector that is no longer registered. Server configuration, not the request. |
   753	| `InvalidRequest` | `credential_required` | The connector requires a credential and none was supplied. |
   754	| `InvalidRequest` | `base_url_invalid` | `base_url` is not a valid URL. |
   755	| `InvalidRequest` | `csv_header_invalid` | The CSV header row is unparsable, or has empty or duplicate column names. |
   756	| `InvalidRequest` | `csv_row_invalid` | A CSV data row could not be parsed. |
   757	| `InvalidRequest` | `csv_empty` | The CSV has a header but no data rows. |
   758	| `InvalidRequest` | `csv_option_column_unknown` | An `option.*` CSV column names an option the template does not declare. |
   759	| `InvalidRequest` | `mode_unknown` | `mode` is not `download` or `print`. |
   760	| `InvalidRequest` | `printer_required` | `mode=print` was requested without a printer. |
   860	| GET | `/api/connections` | List connections (credential redacted) | `200` |
   861	| POST | `/api/connections` | Create a connection (`connector`, `name`, `base_url`, `credential`) | `201` / `400` |
   862	| GET | `/api/connections/{id}` | Connection detail (credential redacted) | `200` / `404` |
   863	| PUT | `/api/connections/{id}` | Update; omitting `credential` keeps the stored one | `200` / `404` |
   864	| DELETE | `/api/connections/{id}` | Delete a connection | `204` / `404` |
   865	
   866	`POST` rejects an unknown `connector`, a missing `credential`, or an invalid `base_url` with `400`.
   867	
   868	**Browse model.** A connector describes its data as a schema and is paged through with browse, then a
   869	selection is turned into label data with materialize.
   870	
   871	- **`GET /connections/{id}/schema`** returns `{ version, resources, relationships }`. A resource is
   872	  `{ id, label, view, columns, filters }`; `view` is `table` or `tree`. Each column (`FieldSpec`) carries
   873	  a `tier`: `cheap` (free from the list call), `hydrated` (needs a per-row fetch), or `derived`
   874	  (computed). Filters (`FilterSpec`) are typed (`search`, `location_id`, `label_id`).
   875	- **`POST /connections/{id}/browse`** takes `{ resource, filters?, parent?, cursor?, page_size? }` and
   876	  returns `{ rows, next_cursor, has_more, count? }`. Each row is `{ id: { resource, key }, cells, url? }`
   877	  (`url` is the row's link to its page in the source system, used to make the name clickable).
   878	  Cursors are opaque, HMAC-signed, and bound to {connector, connection, resource, filter, page}; the
   879	  signing key is per process lifetime, so cursors do not survive a restart (the UI re-browses).
   880	- **`POST /connections/{id}/materialize`** takes `{ rows: [{ resource, key }], fields, expansion }` and
   881	  returns label rows `[{ source, data }]`, where `data` is a string map ready to bind to a template.
   882	
   883	**Egress policy.** All outbound calls go through one shared `reqwest`/rustls client with connect/read
   884	timeouts, an 8 MiB streamed response cap, no redirects, and no proxy-env use; bearer tokens are redacted
   885	from logs and errors. The target IP is allow-checked: loopback, link-local, unspecified, and multicast
   886	are blocked, while private LAN ranges are allowed (the target Homebox commonly lives on the LAN).
   887	Upstream failures surface as `502`; bad filters and budget overruns as `400`.
   888	
   889	**Using a connection (UI).** Settings > Connections adds and edits connections (connector, name, base
   890	URL, API key). The key is write-only: the API returns only `has_credential`, the form shows it as a
   891	password field, and editing with the field left blank keeps the stored key. The Connect page drives the
   892	flow top to bottom: pick a connection (header), then a template and field mapping, then browse the
   893	connector (a generic schema-driven table with typed filters, cursor pagination, and direct drill-down via
   894	relationships) and select rows. Each row's name links to its page in the source system. Selection
   895	persists across filters, drill-down, and resource tabs; a persistent summary shows the whole selection
   896	with a visible/hidden split ("in this view" = currently-loaded rows) plus a reviewable, removable list
   897	grouped by resource, so a bulk add never silently includes unseen rows. Selecting is blocked at the
   898	200-row materialize cap. Materialize turns the selection into label-grid rows; the grid/batch caps at 500
   899	(§2.2). For Homebox specifically, the connector lists items and locations as two flat resources off the
   900	unified `/v1/entities` endpoint (`isLocation=false`/`true`); see [ADR-0021](adr/0021-homebox-connect-hardening.md).
   901	
   902	## Printing
   903	
   904	Architecture: [ADR-0007](adr/0007-printer-architecture-and-transport-model.md). App state (printers,
   905	variables, a job log) lives in SQLite at `{config}/labeler.db` (`LABELER_CONFIG_DIR`, default `/config`),
   906	behind a `store` module.
   907	
   908	- **Printers** are "machine" instances `{ id, name, kind, config }` with an opaque
   909	  per-`kind` JSON `config`, managed via `/printers` CRUD (`id` is a validated slug). `kind` selects a
   910	  `PrinterDriver`; create/replace validate the config for that driver.
   911	- **Printing is driven by `POST /batch` with `mode: print`** (see §2.2). With a `printer`, it builds that
   912	  printer's driver and sends each artifact in the driver's accepted format, recording a job and returning
   913	  a `BatchSummary`. Single templates dispatch one job per label; sheet templates send the paginated PDF
   914	  as one job. Unknown template/printer → 404; transport failures are best-effort and reported per-label
   915	  in the summary. (`POST /print` was removed and absorbed by `/batch`; ADR-0011.)
   916	- **Phase 1 driver:** `cups` sends the rendered PDF over IPP (pure-Rust `ipp` crate, no `lp` binary) to
   917	  a CUPS queue or IPP-Everywhere printer URI. Later families (Zebra ZPL, Brother raster, Dymo) register
   918	  as new drivers without changing dispatch. The cups config schema is:
   919	
   920	  | Field | Type | Required | Notes |
   921	  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
   922	  | `uri` | string | Yes | Must start with `ipp://` or `ipps://`. |
   923	  | `username` | string | No | Used only when `password` is also set. |
   924	  | `password` | string | No | **Write-only.** Never returned in API responses (omitted, not nulled). On `PUT`, omit to keep the stored value; send `null` to clear it; send a string to replace it. Stored plaintext (must be replayed to the printer). |
   925	  | `ca_cert` | string | No | Inline PEM certificate (`-----BEGIN CERTIFICATE-----`). Trusted only for this printer's TLS handshake. Ignored when `insecure` is `true`. |
   926	  | `insecure` | bool | No | Default `false`. When `true`, TLS certificate verification is skipped entirely (`insecure` dominates `ca_cert`). Combining `insecure` with credentials enables MITM credential theft; use only in isolated lab setups. |
   927	  | `render` | object | No | Optional render profile for this printer. See sub-fields below. |
   928	
   929	  `render` sub-fields:
   930	
   931	  | Sub-field | Type | Required | Notes |
   932	  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
   933	  | `color_mode` | `"color"` \| `"bilevel"` | No | Per-field override. Absent: **negotiated** from the printer (see below), not defaulted. `"bilevel"` renders single/tape labels as a 1-bit `image/png` (global luminance threshold, no dithering). Sheet templates always render PDF regardless of this setting. |
   934	  | `resolution` | integer | No | Per-field override for raster DPI, in `[1, 1200]`. Absent: **negotiated** from the printer, else the template `dpi` field. |
   935	
   936	  Bad `render` values (`color_mode` not in the allowed set, `resolution` out of `[1, 1200]` or non-integer)
   937	  are rejected with `422` at printer create (`POST /printers`) or replace (`PUT /printers/{id}`).
   938	
   939	  **Print format selection.** The CUPS driver selects the wire format per job:
   940	
   941	  - A printer with `render.color_mode = "bilevel"` AND a `single`/tape template: renders the label as a
   942	    1-bit `image/png` (the same global-threshold bilevel conversion as `?color_mode=bilevel` on
   943	    `POST /render/label`) and sends it with IPP `document-format: image/png`.
   944	  - All other cases (sheet templates, or a `color` printer): render PDF and send with
   945	    IPP `document-format: application/pdf`.
   946	
   947	  This is slice 2 of [ADR-0033](adr/0033-capability-aware-rendering.md): printer-config-driven format
   948	  selection. The media-width gate is a later slice.
   949	
   950	  **IPP capability auto-negotiation (slice 3).** A printer with no `config.render` is auto-negotiated
   951	  on the print path via IPP `Get-Printer-Attributes`. labeler queries the printer's advertised attributes
   952	  and selects bilevel `image/png` when ALL of the following hold:
   953	
   954	  - The printer advertises a bilevel signal: `print-color-mode-supported` or `print-color-mode-default`
   955	    includes `bi-level`, OR `pwg-raster-document-type-supported` includes `black_1` with no color type
   956	    present in that list.
   957	  - `document-format-supported` includes `image/png`.
   958	  - `printer-resolution-default` reports a square resolution (X and Y DPI equal), expressed in dots per
   959	    inch. labeler uses that reported DPI as the raster resolution.
   960

     1	# Architecture Decision Records
     2	
     3	This directory records the significant architectural decisions for Labeler, using
     4	[Michael Nygard's ADR format](https://cognitect.com/blog/2011/11/15/documenting-architecture-decisions).
     5	
     6	An ADR captures a single decision: its context, the choice made, and the consequences. ADRs are
     7	immutable once **Accepted**: to change a decision, add a new ADR that supersedes the old one (mark the
     8	old one `Superseded by ADR-NNNN` and the new one `Supersedes ADR-NNNN`).
     9	
    10	## Index
    11	
    12	| ADR | Title | Status |

    75	## Adding an ADR
    76	
    77	1. Copy the structure of an existing record. Use the next zero-padded number.
    78	2. Set `Status: Proposed`, fill in Context / Decision / Consequences.
    79	3. Add a row to the index above.
    80	4. On acceptance, set `Status: Accepted` and specify the new behavior as an OpenSpec delta under
    81	   `openspec/changes/<change>/specs/`, which folds into `openspec/specs/` on archive.
    82	   [`../SPEC.md`](../SPEC.md) is frozen (ADR-0057) and is no longer updated.

 succeeded in 0ms:
   840	Passwords must be non-empty (no length minimum). Setting `LABELER_NO_AUTH=true` turns authentication off
   841	for LAN-trust homelab use (never the default): data routes are open via an internal local principal,
   842	but the credential-management endpoints (`/auth/setup`, `/auth/login`, `/auth/logout`, `/auth/password`,
   843	`/users`, `/tokens`) return `403` so no durable credential can be created through the API while auth is
   844	off (the operator `LABELER_INIT_USER` startup bootstrap is unaffected), and a relaxed origin check still
   845	rejects browser drive-by writes with a mismatched `Origin`. `GET /api/auth/me`
   846	then returns `{ authed: true, needsSetup: false, me: { id: "local", username: "local" }, noAuth: true }`,
   847	and the SPA hides the login wall and credential-management UI.
   848	
   849	## 12. Integrations (connectors)
   850	
   851	Decision: [ADR-0018](adr/0018-api-integration-spine.md). A connector pulls label data straight from an
   852	external system of record (first Homebox) so a user can browse and materialize rows instead of re-keying.
   853	
   854	**Connections.** A connection is `{ id, connector, name, base_url, credential, enabled }` stored in
   855	SQLite. The credential (a pasted Homebox API key) is stored as-is for now (at-rest encryption deferred)
   856	and is **never** returned by the API: responses expose only `has_credential`.
   857	
   858	| Method | Path | Purpose | Success |
   859	| --- | --- | --- | --- |
   860	| GET | `/api/connections` | List connections (credential redacted) | `200` |
   861	| POST | `/api/connections` | Create a connection (`connector`, `name`, `base_url`, `credential`) | `201` / `400` |
   862	| GET | `/api/connections/{id}` | Connection detail (credential redacted) | `200` / `404` |
   863	| PUT | `/api/connections/{id}` | Update; omitting `credential` keeps the stored one | `200` / `404` |
   864	| DELETE | `/api/connections/{id}` | Delete a connection | `204` / `404` |
   865	
   866	`POST` rejects an unknown `connector`, a missing `credential`, or an invalid `base_url` with `400`.
   867	
   868	**Browse model.** A connector describes its data as a schema and is paged through with browse, then a
   869	selection is turned into label data with materialize.
   870

/home/linuxbrew/.linuxbrew/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba docs/adr/0018-api-integration-spine.md | sed -n '1,90p'" in /home/pfa/projects/labeler/.worktrees/issue-169
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# 18. API integration spine (connectors)
     2	
     3	**Status:** Accepted
     4	
     5	## Context
     6	
     7	Labeler renders labels from data the user types or imports as CSV. The M7 milestone adds a way to pull
     8	that data straight from an external system of record (first Homebox, an inventory manager) so a user can
     9	browse their items and materialize them into label rows without re-keying. That requires outbound HTTP
    10	from the service, a uniform way to describe and page through a third party's data, and a place to store
    11	per-connection credentials. This ADR records the design of that integration spine: egress, the connector
    12	framework, browse cursors, the connections store, and the Homebox connector.
    13	
    14	## Decision
    15	
    16	- **Hardened egress.** All outbound HTTP goes through one shared `reqwest` client (rustls). Before each
    17	  request the target IP is allow-checked: loopback, link-local, unspecified, and multicast are blocked,
    18	  but private LAN ranges are allowed (the target Homebox commonly lives on the same LAN). The client sets
    19	  connect and read timeouts, caps the response body by streaming with an 8 MiB ceiling, disables
    20	  redirects, ignores proxy environment variables, and redacts bearer tokens from any error or log output.
    21	  **Residual risk:** the IP check resolves DNS and then reqwest re-resolves on connect, leaving a
    22	  resolve-then-connect TOCTOU window for DNS rebinding. This is accepted for a single-tenant, authed,
    23	  LAN-facing tool. The future tightening, if needed, is a custom `reqwest::dns::Resolve` resolver that
    24	  pins the checked address.
    25	
    26	- **Connector framework.** A connector exposes a `Connector`-shaped surface (schema, browse,
    27	  materialize). Because there is exactly one connector today, dispatch goes through an enum registry
    28	  (`Connectors` / `ConnectorRegistry`) rather than `dyn` plus an async-trait, avoiding boxing and
    29	  async-trait machinery for a single variant. The browse model is `schema` (resources, tiered fields,
    30	  filters, relationships) then `browse` (paged display rows) then `materialize` (selected rows to label
    31	  data). Fields carry a tier: `Cheap` (free from the list call), `Hydrated` (needs a per-row fetch),
    32	  `Derived` (computed).
    33	
    34	- **Browse cursors.** Pagination cursors are opaque to the client, HMAC-SHA256 signed, and bound to the
    35	  tuple {connector, connection, resource, filter_hash, page}. The signing key is generated per process
    36	  lifetime, so cursors do not survive a restart; the UI simply re-browses from the first page. This keeps
    37	  cursors unforgeable and self-describing without a server-side cursor table.
    38	
    39	- **Connections store.** Connections live in a `connections` table (connector, name, base_url,
    40	  credential, enabled). The credential is a pasted Homebox API key, stored as-is for now (at-rest
    41	  encryption deferred, consistent with ADR-0017). The credential is **never** returned by the API:
    42	  responses expose only `has_credential` so the UI can show whether one is set.
    43	
    44	- **Homebox connector.** Talks to Homebox's unified `/v1/entities` API, verified against the Homebox
    45	  swagger: list via `q` / `page` / `pageSize` / `tags` / `parentIds` as repeated bare-key query params
    46	  returning an `{ items, total }` envelope, tree via `/v1/entities/tree`, single entity via
    47	  `/v1/entities/{id}`, and field discovery via `/v1/entities/fields` (a string array). Auth is
    48	  `Authorization: Bearer <key>` with the key pasted verbatim, since a Homebox key already carries its
    49	  `hb_` prefix.
    50	
    51	## Consequences
    52	
    53	- The service now makes outbound requests; deployments must allow egress to the configured Homebox host.
    54	  The egress allow-check intentionally permits private ranges, so it does not protect against a malicious
    55	  operator-supplied `base_url` on the LAN. That is acceptable under single-tenant, authed operation.
    56	- New API surface: `GET/POST /api/connections`, `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/connections/{id}`, and
    57	  `GET /api/connections/{id}/schema`, `POST /api/connections/{id}/browse`,
    58	  `POST /api/connections/{id}/materialize`. Upstream failures map to `502`; bad filters and budget
    59	  overruns map to `400`.
    60	- Cursors are not durable across restarts by design; clients must treat them as ephemeral and re-browse.
    61	- **Deferred:** credential encryption at rest, additional connectors, a `dyn` connector surface (revisit
    62	  once a second connector exists), and a rebind-proof pinning resolver. Any of these can supersede this
    63	  ADR when revisited.

docs/adr/0060-connection-scoped-field-transforms.md:19:**Field transforms are connection-scoped regular expressions with named capture groups that derive virtual fields across all connector read paths.**
docs/adr/0060-connection-scoped-field-transforms.md:21:**1. Connection-scoped configuration.** Transforms are configured per-connection in `ConnectionInput` and stored on the `Connection` record as a list of rules: `{ resource, source, pattern }`. They belong to the connection, keeping templates decoupled and portable.
docs/adr/0060-connection-scoped-field-transforms.md:27:**4. Save-time validation against static connector descriptors.** Connectors expose static `ResourceDescriptor`s defining their resource identifiers, static columns, and dynamic prefix support (e.g., `custom:` for Homebox). Save-time validation (`validate_transforms`) rejects invalid regular expressions, missing named groups, source fields that do not exist in the descriptor, target names colliding with declared columns, duplicate derived names on the same resource, and reserved namespace prefixes (`datetime`, `datetime.*`, `vars.*`). Violations fail with `400 InvalidRequest` and reason `connection_transform_invalid`, identifying the zero-based rule index in the message.
docs/adr/0060-connection-scoped-field-transforms.md:29:**5. Inert behavior on resource withdrawal.** If a connector subsequently retires a resource that a stored transform references, the transform becomes inert across `schema`, `browse`, and `materialize` without breaking requests or causing errors.
docs/adr/0060-connection-scoped-field-transforms.md:34:  - `materialize`: Rewrites the requested field list to request source fields from the connector, runs the connector, evaluates transforms on `data`, and projects results back to exactly the caller's requested field set.
docs/adr/0060-connection-scoped-field-transforms.md:38:- Templates can bind directly to derived field names (e.g. `{location_id}`) exactly like native connector fields.
docs/adr/0060-connection-scoped-field-transforms.md:39:- Upstream connector code remains focused on HTTP transport and standard mapping, while the `Connectors` wrapper uniformizes derived field handling across all connectors.
docs/adr/0060-connection-scoped-field-transforms.md:42:- Stored transforms are transparently returned on `ConnectionView` alongside non-sensitive connection details.

openspec/changes/archive/2026-08-21-issue-161-connector-field-transforms/review-raw-1.txt:5:- Materialize injection is underspecified against the current Homebox implementation. `design.md` says the wrapper “adds” the rule source to the fields passed down, then strips unrequested sources (`design.md:75-80`). But `HomeboxConnector::materialize` inserts a value for every requested field (`src/connector/homebox.rs:317-320`), and `extract_field` returns `String::new()` for unknown keys (`src/connector/homebox.rs:468-472`). If the wrapper passes a requested derived field such as `location_id` through to Homebox, Homebox will produce `location_id: ""`; on a no-match path the spec requires the derived key to be absent, not empty (`spec.md:210-217`). The design must say derived field names are removed before calling the connector, sources are injected separately, and the final `data` is projected back to requested fields only.
openspec/changes/archive/2026-08-21-issue-161-connector-field-transforms/review-raw-1.txt:7:- The “source must be declared text” rule does not eliminate browse/materialize divergence. Homebox declares text fields with `Tier::Hydrated` (`manufacturer`, `modelNumber`, `serialNumber`) in schema (`src/connector/homebox.rs:127-134`), but browse only includes those cells when the list response happens to contain them (`src/connector/homebox.rs:399-407`), while materialize fetches the detail row and extracts requested fields from it (`src/connector/homebox.rs:309-320`). The spec promises browse-derived cells show what materialize will produce (`spec.md:154-157`), but also says browse will not fetch missing sources and will simply not match (`spec.md:159-161`); the design acknowledges hydrated sources cannot be satisfied in browse (`design.md:148-150`). Either restrict transform sources to fields guaranteed available in browse, or remove the browse/materialize equivalence claim.
openspec/changes/archive/2026-08-21-issue-161-connector-field-transforms/review-raw-1.txt:15:- Dynamic custom sources weaken the stated “declared TEXT field” validation. Homebox concrete custom fields are discovered by a live schema call (`src/connector/homebox.rs:138-148`), while the design proposes validating any `custom:` prefix without contacting upstream (`design.md:48-57`, `design.md:59-61`). That means `custom:Intenral SKU` can pass save-time validation even though the connector has not declared it. If wildcard custom sources are intended, the spec should say that; otherwise custom sources need a different validation rule.
openspec/changes/archive/2026-08-21-issue-161-connector-field-transforms/review-raw-1.txt:21:- The `PUT` behavior is implementable, but the plan should state validation uses the stored connector, not `body.connector`: current `PUT /connections/{id}` validates URL and updates name/base/public URL/credential/enabled, but never reads `body.connector` (`src/api.rs:1243-1278`), and `Store::update_connection` has no connector argument (`src/store.rs:675-683`).
openspec/changes/archive/2026-08-21-issue-161-connector-field-transforms/review-raw-1.txt:23:- The claim that regex group names cannot collide with Homebox `custom:<name>` keys is correct for the current regex grammar: `:` is not a valid group-name character (`regex-syntax-0.8.11/src/ast/parse.rs:111-116`), while Homebox custom keys are built as `custom:{name}` (`src/connector/homebox.rs:143-148`, `src/connector/homebox.rs:454-466`).
openspec/changes/archive/2026-08-21-issue-161-connector-field-transforms/review-raw-1.txt:35:1. Amend materialize design/spec to remove requested derived names before connector calls, inject only required sources, then project final output back to requested fields while preserving absent-on-no-match.

openspec/changes/issue-169-connection-public-url/review.md:24:- `design.md` says the new ADR “supersedes nothing”, but ADR-0018 already owns the connection store shape and Homebox connector architecture. Leaving ADR-0018 accepted and unsuperseded would preserve an accepted record saying connections are `(connector, name, base_url, credential, enabled)` while the new behavior adds `public_url` and gives it link-generation semantics. openspec/changes/issue-169-connection-public-url/review.md:26:- The `public_url_invalid` plan misses the existing reason-table completeness test. The spec extends frozen §10.1 through OpenSpec, but `src/errors.rs` currently reads only `docs/SPEC.md` and compares that table to `Reason::ALL`; adding `Reason::PublicUrlInvalid` will fail unless the test is updated to account for OpenSpec specs, since `docs/SPEC.md` must not be edited. Evidence: `openspec/changes/issue-169-connection-public-url/specs/connections/spec.md:76-80`, `openspec/changes/issue-169-connection-public-url/proposal.md:53-54`, `src/errors.rs:550-589`, `AGENTS.md:12-17`.
openspec/changes/issue-169-connection-public-url/review.md:28:- The `PUT /api/connections/{id}` contract is ambiguous around `connector`. The spec says update accepts “the same payload as create”, and create rejects an unknown connector, but the update handler never reads or validates `body.connector`; it only validates `base_url`, maps `public_url`, and updates name/base URL/public URL/credential/enabled. The plan needs to state the actual post-change contract: connector is required by the input type but not mutable, and whether mismatched/unknown connector values are ignored or rejected. Evidence: `openspec/changes/issue-169-connection-public-url/specs/connections/spec.md:28-31`, `openspec/changes/issue-169-connection-public-url/specs/connections/spec.md:48-51`, `src/api.rs:1093-1102`, `src/api.rs:1167-1171`, `src/api.rs:1243-1272`.
openspec/changes/issue-169-connection-public-url/review.md:34:- Decide explicitly whether `public_url` may contain URL userinfo. Current validation would allow `https://user:pass@example.com`, and the new contract only forbids non-http(s), missing host, query, and fragment. Since this value is printed into QR codes and shown as a browser link, the allowance should be intentional. Evidence: `openspec/changes/issue-169-connection-public-url/specs/connections/spec.md:73-77`, `src/api.rs:1116-1139`.
openspec/changes/issue-169-connection-public-url/review.md:36:- Add explicit test-impact notes for existing server tests that currently assert invalid `public_url` returns `base_url_invalid`; they must move to `public_url_invalid` with the planned reason change. /home/linuxbrew/.linuxbrew/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba openspec/changes/issue-169-connection-public-url/specs/connections/spec.md | sed -n '1,260p'" in /home/pfa/projects/labeler/.worktrees/issue-169
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	## Purpose
     2	
     3	Defines the connection record that binds Labeler to an upstream inventory system, its CRUD contract,
     4	and the two addresses a connection carries: the base URL Labeler fetches from and the optional public
     5	URL that Labeler puts into the links and QR codes it generates for people.
     6	
     7	## ADDED Requirements
     8	
     9	### Requirement: Connection record
    10	
    11	A connection SHALL be `{ id, connector, name, base_url, public_url, transforms, credential, enabled }`,
    12	persisted by the server. `public_url` SHALL be optional and default to absent. The credential SHALL
    13	never be returned by any endpoint; responses SHALL expose it only as the boolean `has_credential`;
    14	`transforms` SHALL be returned in full.
    15	
    16	This requirement supersedes the connection record shape in the frozen `docs/SPEC.md` §12
    17	("Integrations (connectors)"). Every other part of §12 remains authoritative. `transforms` is named
    18	here only so the record is stated whole: every rule about what a transform is, when it is accepted,
    19	and what it derives belongs to `connector-field-transforms`, which this requirement neither restates
    20	nor alters.
    21	
    22	`GET /api/connections` SHALL list every connection ordered by `name`; `GET /api/connections/{id}`
    23	SHALL return one, or `404` when the id is unknown.
    24	
    25	#### Scenario: Reading a connection
    26	
    27	- **WHEN** a client reads a connection through `GET /api/connections` or `GET /api/connections/{id}`
    28	- **THEN** the response contains `id`, `connector`, `name`, `base_url`, `public_url`, `enabled`, and
    29	  `has_credential`
    30	- **AND** it contains no credential value
    31	- **AND** `public_url` is `null` when the connection has none
    32	
    33	#### Scenario: Reading a connection that does not exist
    34	
    35	- **WHEN** a client requests `GET /api/connections/{id}` for an unknown id
    36	- **THEN** the response is `404`
    37	
    38	### Requirement: Creating a connection
    39	
    40	`POST /api/connections` SHALL accept
    41	`{ connector, name, base_url, public_url?, transforms?, credential, enabled? }` and return `201` with
    42	the created connection. A missing, `null`, or blank `public_url` SHALL store no
    43	public URL. A missing `enabled` SHALL default to `true`. An unknown `connector` SHALL be rejected with
    44	`400` and reason `connector_unknown`; a `credential` that is missing, `null`, or empty SHALL be
    45	rejected with `400` and reason `credential_required`.
    46	
    47	This requirement supersedes the `POST /api/connections` payload description in the frozen
    48	`docs/SPEC.md` §12, alongside `connector-field-transforms`, which already superseded it to the extent
    49	of adding `transforms`. The handling of `transforms` on create stays that capability's.
    50	
    51	#### Scenario: Created with a public URL
    52	
    53	- **WHEN** a client posts a connection whose `public_url` is `https://homebox.example.com`
    54	- **THEN** the response is `201` and its `public_url` is `https://homebox.example.com`
    55	
    56	#### Scenario: Created without a public URL
    57	
    58	- **WHEN** a client posts a connection that omits `public_url`, or sends it as `null` or `""`
    59	- **THEN** the response is `201` and its `public_url` is `null`
    60	
    61	#### Scenario: Created without a credential
    62	
    63	- **WHEN** a client posts a connection whose `credential` is missing, `null`, or `""`
    64	- **THEN** the response is `400` with `details.reason` `credential_required`
    65	
    66	#### Scenario: Created with an unknown connector
    67	
    68	- **WHEN** a client posts a connection whose `connector` is not a registered connector
    69	- **THEN** the response is `400` with `details.reason` `connector_unknown`
    70	
    71	### Requirement: Updating a connection
    72	
    73	`PUT /api/connections/{id}` SHALL accept the same payload shape as create and return `200` with the
    74	updated connection, or `404` when the id is unknown. It SHALL update `name`, `base_url`, `public_url`,
    75	`enabled`, and, when one is supplied, `credential`. `connector` SHALL remain a required key of the
    76	payload but SHALL NOT be applied: a connection's connector is fixed at creation, and a payload naming
    77	a different or unknown connector SHALL neither change it nor fail the request. Omitting `credential`,
    78	or sending it empty, SHALL keep the stored credential. For `public_url` the three input forms SHALL be
    79	distinguished: omitting the key keeps the stored value, sending `null` or a blank string clears it,
    80	and sending a URL replaces it. `transforms` is accepted on update under the rules stated by
    81	`connector-field-transforms`.
    82	
    83	This requirement supersedes the `PUT /api/connections/{id}` description in the frozen
    84	`docs/SPEC.md` §12, alongside `connector-field-transforms` for the `transforms` key.
    85	
    86	#### Scenario: Omitting public_url keeps the stored one
    87	
    88	- **WHEN** a client updates a connection that has a public URL, with a payload that omits `public_url`
    89	- **THEN** the response is `200` and the connection still has its previous `public_url`
    90	
    91	#### Scenario: Blanking public_url clears it
    92	
    93	- **WHEN** a client updates a connection that has a public URL, sending `public_url` as `null` or `""`
    94	- **THEN** the response is `200` and the connection's `public_url` is `null`
    95	
    96	#### Scenario: Setting a new public_url
    97	
    98	- **WHEN** a client updates a connection sending `public_url` as `https://homebox.example.com/`
    99	- **THEN** the response is `200` and the connection's `public_url` is `https://homebox.example.com`
   100	
   101	#### Scenario: Connector in an update payload is ignored
   102	
   103	- **WHEN** a client updates a connection sending a `connector` that differs from the stored one
   104	- **THEN** the response is `200` and the connection's `connector` is unchanged
   105	
   106	#### Scenario: Updating a connection that does not exist
   107	
   108	- **WHEN** a client updates an unknown id
   109	- **THEN** the response is `404`
   110	
   111	### Requirement: Deleting a connection
   112	
   113	`DELETE /api/connections/{id}` SHALL delete the connection and return `204`, or `404` when the id is
   114	unknown.
   115	
   116	This requirement supersedes the `DELETE /api/connections/{id}` row of the frozen `docs/SPEC.md` §12.
   117	
   118	#### Scenario: Deleting a connection
   119	
   120	- **WHEN** a client deletes an existing connection
   121	- **THEN** the response is `204` and the connection no longer appears in `GET /api/connections`
   122	
   123	#### Scenario: Deleting a connection that does not exist
   124	
   125	- **WHEN** a client deletes an unknown id
   126	- **THEN** the response is `404`
   127	
   128	### Requirement: Connection URL validation
   129	
   130	`base_url` and `public_url` SHALL each be validated the same way before storage: surrounding
   131	whitespace trimmed, parseable as an absolute URL, scheme `http` or `https`, a host present, and no
   132	query string, fragment, or embedded userinfo (`user:pass@`). Userinfo is rejected because a connection
   133	URL is printed into QR codes and rendered as a link, so credentials carried in it would end up on a
   134	physical label. Every trailing `/` SHALL be trimmed before storage, so `https://host/sub/path///` and
   135	`https://host/sub/path` store the same value. A rejected value SHALL produce `400` with
   136	`details.reason` `base_url_invalid` for `base_url` and `public_url_invalid` for `public_url`, and a
   137	message naming the rejected field. A save SHALL also be rejected for a bad transform, under the rules
   138	and the `connection_transform_invalid` reason stated by `connector-field-transforms`.
   139	
   140	This requirement supersedes the `base_url_invalid` row of the reason table in the frozen
   141	`docs/SPEC.md` §10.1 and extends it with `public_url_invalid`. The other rows remain authoritative.
   142	
   143	#### Scenario: Rejecting a malformed public URL
   144	
   145	- **WHEN** a client sends `public_url` as `not a url`, `ftp://host`, `http://`, `https://host?x=1`,
   146	  `https://host#f`, or `https://user:pass@host`
   147	- **THEN** the response is `400` with `details.reason` `public_url_invalid`
   148	
   149	#### Scenario: Rejecting a malformed base URL
   150	
   151	- **WHEN** a client sends `base_url` as `not a url` or `https://user:pass@host`
   152	- **THEN** the response is `400` with `details.reason` `base_url_invalid`
   153	
   154	#### Scenario: Normalizing a stored URL
   155	
   156	- **WHEN** a client sends `base_url` as `  http://homebox.lan:7745/  `
   157	- **THEN** the stored and returned value is `http://homebox.lan:7745`
   158	
   159	### Requirement: Generated links use the public URL
   160	
   161	Every URL Labeler generates for a person to open SHALL be built from the connection's `public_url`
   162	when it has one, and from `base_url` otherwise. This covers the `url` on a browsed row and the
   163	`item_url` and `location_url` fields materialized into label data, which is what a printed QR code
   164	encodes. A blank or whitespace-only stored `public_url` SHALL behave as absent. Requests Labeler
   165	itself makes to the upstream system SHALL always use `base_url`, never `public_url`.
   166	
   167	This requirement supersedes nothing in `docs/SPEC.md`, which does not specify how entity links are
   168	built.
   169	
   170	#### Scenario: Public URL set
   171	
   172	- **WHEN** a connection has `base_url` `http://homebox:7745` and `public_url`
   173	  `https://homebox.example.com`, and a client browses or materializes a row for entity `e1`
   174	- **THEN** the row's `url` and any materialized `item_url` or `location_url` are
   175	  `https://homebox.example.com/entity/e1`
   176	- **AND** the upstream request Labeler made went to `http://homebox:7745`
   177	
   178	#### Scenario: Public URL absent
   179	
   180	- **WHEN** a connection has `base_url` `http://homebox:7745` and no `public_url`, and a client browses
   181	  or materializes a row for entity `e1`
   182	- **THEN** the row's `url` and any materialized `item_url` or `location_url` are
   183	  `http://homebox:7745/entity/e1`
   184	
   185	### Requirement: Connections settings UI
   186	
   187	Settings > Connections SHALL let an operator set and clear a connection's public URL. The connection
   188	form SHALL carry an optional **public url** field alongside **base url**, pre-filled from the stored
   189	value, and SHALL submit `public_url` on every save so that clearing the field clears the stored value.
   190	A non-blank entry SHALL be rejected in the form, without a request, when it is not a parseable
   191	`http`/`https` URL. The connections table SHALL show each connection's public URL, and `-` when it has
   192	none.
   193	
   194	This requirement supersedes the "Using a connection (UI)" paragraph of the frozen `docs/SPEC.md` §12
   195	as far as it describes the connection form's fields. The rest of that paragraph, covering the Connect
   196	page, remains authoritative.
   197	
   198	#### Scenario: Setting a public URL
   199	
   200	- **WHEN** the operator edits a connection, types `https://homebox.example.com` into **public url**,
   201	  and saves
   202	- **THEN** the request body carries `public_url: "https://homebox.example.com"`
   203	- **AND** the table row shows that public URL
   204	
   205	#### Scenario: Clearing a public URL
   206	
   207	- **WHEN** the operator edits a connection that has a public URL, empties the **public url** field,
   208	  and saves
   209	- **THEN** the request body carries `public_url: null`
   210	- **AND** the table row shows `-`
   211	
   212	#### Scenario: Rejecting an invalid public URL in the form
   213	
   214	- **WHEN** the operator types `homebox.example.com` into **public url** and saves
   215	- **THEN** the form shows a validation error and sends no request
   216	
   217	#### Scenario: Leaving a public URL unset
   218	
   219	- **WHEN** the operator adds a connection and leaves **public url** empty
   220	- **THEN** the request body carries `public_url: null` and the connection is created

/home/linuxbrew/.linuxbrew/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba openspec/changes/issue-169-connection-public-url/proposal.md | sed -n '1,120p'" in /home/pfa/projects/labeler/.worktrees/issue-169
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	## Why
     2	
     3	Implements [#169](https://github.com/pfa230/labeler/issues/169). When Labeler reaches Homebox over a
     4	Docker network or an internal reverse proxy, `base_url` holds an address only the server can resolve
     5	(`http://homebox:7745`). Every entity link Labeler emits is built from that address, so printed QR
     6	codes and Connect-page row links resolve to a host a phone or a client browser cannot reach.
     7	
     8	The server half of the fix already landed (`public_url` on the connection record, validated in the
     9	connection endpoints, used by the Homebox connector for link generation), and the UI's API types
    10	already declare the field (`ui/src/api/connectors.ts:15`, `:24`). What is missing is the only part an
    11	operator can touch: Settings > Connections renders no input for it, so no connection can ever be given
    12	one. This change adds that field and writes down the contract the server already implements, which no
    13	spec or ADR records today.
    14	
    15	## What Changes
    16	
    17	- Settings > Connections gains an optional **public url** field on the connection form, next to
    18	  **base url**. Leaving it blank means "no public URL"; the connection then keeps deriving links from
    19	  `base_url`, as before.
    20	- Saving the form always sends `public_url`, so blanking a stored value clears it and typing one sets
    21	  it, exactly as the form already treats `transforms`. The UI validates it the way it validates
    22	  `base_url` (parseable, `http`/`https`), and only when it is non-blank.
    23	- The connections table gains a **Public URL** column so an operator can see which connections
    24	  override link generation without opening each form.
    25	- The connection contract is written down as an OpenSpec capability: the record's shape, the
    26	  create/update/read/delete semantics including `public_url`'s omit-keeps / null-clears behavior on
    27	  `PUT`, URL validation and normalization, and the rule that `public_url` governs *generated links*
    28	  while `base_url` remains the only address Labeler fetches from.
    29	- A rejected `public_url` reports `details.reason = "public_url_invalid"` instead of borrowing
    30	  `base_url_invalid`, so a client can tell which of the two URL fields failed.
    31	- Both connection URLs reject embedded userinfo (`https://user:pass@host`). A connection URL is
    32	  printed into QR codes, so credentials carried in one end up on a physical label. **BREAKING** for
    33	  the narrow case of an existing `base_url` that carries userinfo: it is untouched at rest, but the
    34	  next save of that connection is rejected until the credentials are removed.
    35	- One new ADR records the load-bearing decision: `public_url` is the link base, `base_url` is the
    36	  fetch base.
    37	- Otherwise not breaking: `public_url` stays optional, and a connection without one behaves exactly as
    38	  it does today.
    39	
    40	## Capabilities
    41	
    42	### New Capabilities
    43	- `connections`: the connection record and its CRUD contract, the `public_url` field, and the rule
    44	  that generated entity links use the public URL while upstream requests use the base URL. Supersedes
    45	  the connection portions of the frozen `docs/SPEC.md` §12 (the record shape, the `POST`/`PUT`/`DELETE`
    46	  contract, and "Using a connection (UI)" as it describes the connection form), and the
    47	  `base_url_invalid` row of the §10.1 reason table. Where `openspec/specs/connector-field-transforms/`
    48	  already superseded those same §12 sentences to the extent of adding `transforms`, this capability
    49	  carries `transforms` in the record and leaves every rule about it to that capability.
    50	
    51	### Modified Capabilities
    52	- None. `connector-field-transforms` keeps sole ownership of the transform rules; this change neither
    53	  restates nor alters them.
    54	
    55	## Impact
    56	
    57	- `ui/src/pages/settings/ConnectionsSection.tsx`: the form field, its validation, the submit payload,
    58	  and the table column.
    59	- `ui/src/pages/settings/ConnectionsSection.test.tsx`: coverage for setting, clearing, and rejecting
    60	  a public URL.
    61	- `src/reason.rs` and `src/api.rs`: the new `public_url_invalid` reason, the userinfo rejection, and
    62	  the validation call sites that raise them. `src/lib.rs` and `src/api.rs` tests that currently assert
    63	  `base_url_invalid` for a rejected `public_url` move to the new slug.
    64	- `docs/adr/`: one new ADR plus its row in `docs/adr/README.md`, and a note on the ADR-0018 row that
    65	  its connections-store decision is superseded in part.
    66	- `openspec/specs/connections/spec.md` on archive.
    67	- No change to `ui/src/api/connectors.ts`, which already declares `public_url`, or to `src/errors.rs`,
    68	  whose reason-documentation test already accepts a slug documented in an OpenSpec spec
    69	  (`src/errors.rs:577-600`).
    70	- `src/store.rs` and `src/connector/homebox.rs` already implement the rest of the specified server
    71	  contract; the change verifies them against the new spec rather than editing them.

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	## Context
     2	
     3	See `proposal.md` for motivation and `specs/connections/spec.md` for the contract. The relevant
     4	current state, on this branch after merging `main` at `c37f80e`:
     5	
     6	- The server already stores and honours `public_url`. `src/store.rs:52` carries it on `Connection`,
     7	  the migration `ALTER TABLE connections ADD COLUMN public_url TEXT` is shipped (`src/store.rs:167`),
     8	  and `UpdateConnection` carries it as an `UpdateField<String>` so a `PUT` can keep, clear, or set it
     9	  (`src/store.rs:73`, applied in `update_connection` at `src/store.rs:699`).
    10	- `src/api.rs:1073` exposes it on `ConnectionView`; `ConnectionInput` models the three input forms as
    11	  `Option<Option<String>>` (`src/api.rs:1101`) via the shared `deserialize_optional_field`
    12	  (`src/api.rs:1110`), and the handlers translate that into `UpdateField`.
    13	- `src/connector/homebox.rs:128` resolves the link base with `external_base_url`, falling back to
    14	  `base_url` when `public_url` is absent or whitespace, and threads it through `browse` and
    15	  `materialize`. Upstream fetches go through `base(conn)` (`src/connector/homebox.rs:123`), which
    16	  reads `base_url` only.
    17	- `ui/src/api/connectors.ts:15` and `:24` already declare `public_url` on `Connection` and
    18	  `ConnectionInput`. `ConnectionsSection.tsx` mentions it nowhere: the form has no input for it
    19	  (fields end at the enabled checkbox), the submit payload omits it (`ConnectionsSection.tsx:52-59`),
    20	  and the table has no column for it (`:254-259`, row cells at `:204-210`). That is the whole gap.
    21	- `validate_and_normalize_url` (`src/api.rs:1122`) takes a field name for the message but hardcodes
    22	  `Reason::BaseUrlInvalid`, so a rejected `public_url` reports the wrong discriminator. It checks
    23	  scheme, host, query, and fragment, and does not check userinfo.
    24	- #161 landed just before this change and moved two things this plan depended on. The connection
    25	  record now also carries `transforms`, and `openspec/specs/connector-field-transforms/spec.md:31-35`
    26	  already supersedes the frozen §12 record sentence "to the extent of adding `transforms`". And
    27	  `src/errors.rs:577-600` now scans `openspec/` for backticked reason slugs, so a reason documented in
    28	  an OpenSpec spec rather than in the frozen §10.1 table already satisfies the reason-documentation
    29	  test. Neither needs doing here.
    30	
    31	## Goals / Non-Goals
    32	
    33	**Goals:**
    34	
    35	- Make the shipped server capability reachable from Settings > Connections.
    36	- Record the connection contract in `openspec/specs/`, where only the transform slice of it lives.
    37	- Make the error discriminator name the field that actually failed.
    38	
    39	**Non-Goals:**
    40	
    41	- Probing or reachability-checking the public URL. It is by definition an address the server may not
    42	  be able to resolve, so validation stays syntactic.
    43	- Per-template or per-render URL overrides. The `qr_base_url` variable already covers that case and is
    44	  untouched here.
    45	- Any change to how Labeler fetches from upstream, to cursors, to transforms, or to the connector
    46	  schema.
    47	- A second connector. Only Homebox exists; the requirement is written in connector-neutral terms but
    48	  only Homebox implements it.
    49	
    50	## Decisions
    51	
    52	**A connection carries two addresses with distinct jobs, and the ADR says so.** `base_url` is the
    53	address Labeler dials; `public_url` is the address Labeler prints. This change adds
    54	**ADR-0061, "A connection's public URL is the link base, its base URL is the fetch base"**, plus its
    55	row in `docs/adr/README.md`. 0059 and 0060 are taken by the changes that merged while this one was
    56	being planned, so 0061 is the next free number.
    57	
    58	It supersedes the **Connections store** decision of
    59	[ADR-0018](../../../docs/adr/0018-api-integration-spine.md), which records the connection row as
    60	`(connector, name, base_url, credential, enabled)`
    61	(`docs/adr/0018-api-integration-spine.md:39-42`) and would otherwise stand as an accepted record
    62	contradicting the field this change makes reachable. Only that decision is superseded: ADR-0018's
    63	browse-cursor, connector-schema, and Homebox-endpoint decisions stand, so ADR-0018 keeps the status
    64	`Accepted` with its row noting the partial supersession. ADR-0060 amended the same record from the
    65	transform side without superseding ADR-0018; both amendments stand and neither contradicts the other.
    66	The `public_url` server work itself landed in commits `d18ebad`, `2eafccf`, `78504a3` on 2026-08-17
    67	with no ADR at all, which is the gap this closes.
    68	
    69	*Alternative considered:* one URL plus a rewrite rule (store only `base_url` and a host substitution
    70	applied at link time). Rejected: it encodes the same information less directly, and an operator
    71	reasoning about "what does the QR code point at?" would have to apply the rule mentally.
    72	
    73	**Clearing is expressed as `null`, and the form always sends the key.** The API distinguishes
    74	key-absent (keep) from `null`/blank (clear), which exists for API clients doing partial updates. The
    75	form is not a partial update: it renders every field, so it always sends `public_url`, as `null` when
    76	the input is blank. That makes "empty the box and save" clear the stored value, which is the only
    77	behavior an operator would predict from a text input, and it is how the form already treats
    78	`transforms` (`ConnectionsSection.tsx:57`).
    79	
    80	*Alternative considered:* omit the key when blank. Rejected: it makes the field write-once from the
    81	UI, with no way to undo a public URL short of a hand-written request.
    82	
    83	**Field-typed URL validation.** `validate_and_normalize_url` gains a small `UrlField` enum
    84	(`Base`/`Public`) in place of its `&str` field name, with the wire name and the `Reason` hanging off
    85	it. One value decides both the message text and the discriminator, so the two cannot drift, and the
    86	call sites stop passing a bare string that controls neither. `Reason::PublicUrlInvalid =>
    87	"public_url_invalid"` joins the `reasons!` macro list in `src/reason.rs`; adding a slug is additive
    88	and breaks no client, since only renames are breaking (`src/reason.rs:3`). No test scaffolding is
    89	needed for the new slug: `src/errors.rs:577-600` already counts a slug as documented when an OpenSpec
    90	spec names it in backticks, and `specs/connections/spec.md` names this one.
    91	
    92	*Alternative considered:* keep the `&str` parameter and add a `Reason` parameter beside it. Rejected:
    93	two parameters that must agree is exactly the drift the enum removes.
    94	
    95	*Alternative considered:* leave the discriminator as `base_url_invalid`. Rejected as recorded in this
    96	change: a machine-readable reason that names the wrong field is worse than no distinction at all, and
    97	the UI's own error text comes from `message`, so nothing else compensates.
    98	
    99	**Userinfo is rejected in both URL fields.** A connection URL is printed into QR codes and rendered as
   100	a browser link, so `https://user:pass@homebox.example.com` would put credentials on a physical label.
   101	Rejecting it on `public_url` alone would leave two fields with two validation rules and one spec
   102	carrying the exception, so the rule applies to both. It bites only at write time: a stored `base_url`
   103	with userinfo keeps working until someone saves that connection.
   104	
   105	*Alternative considered:* accept userinfo and document the hazard. Rejected as recorded in this
   106	change; validation that already checks scheme, host, query, and fragment has no reason to wave through
   107	the one component that leaks a secret.
   108	
   109	**The UI validates the public URL exactly as it validates the base URL, and only when non-blank.**
   110	`ConnectionForm` already parses `base_url` with `new URL()` and checks the protocol before sending
   111	(`ConnectionsSection.tsx:47-49`); the public URL reuses that shape so both fields fail the same way,
   112	in the form, without a round trip. Blank skips validation entirely, because blank is a legal value
   113	meaning "none".
   114	
   115	**The table gets a Public URL column.** The form is per-connection; the column answers "which of these
   116	print external links?" at a glance, which is the question an operator debugging a scanned QR code
   117	actually has. An unset value renders `-`, matching the table's habit of rendering a short token
   118	(`set`/`none`, `yes`/`no`) rather than an empty cell.
   119	
   120	**The `connections` capability names `transforms` but owns nothing about it.** Writing the record whole
   121	is what the first-touch rule asks for, and leaving `transforms` out would make the record wrong the day
   122	it is archived. Restating its rules would duplicate `connector-field-transforms` and invite the two to
   123	drift, so the record requirement names the field and points at that capability for every rule.
   124	
   125	*Alternative considered:* a `MODIFIED` delta against `connector-field-transforms`. Rejected: that
   126	capability's requirement is about transforms, not about the connection record or its URLs; widening it
   127	to carry `public_url` would put two unrelated contracts in one requirement.
   128	
   129	**`connector` on `PUT` is documented as inert, not made strict.** The update handler never reads
   130	`body.connector`, so a payload naming a different or unknown connector is accepted and changes nothing,
   131	while `POST` rejects an unknown one. The spec records that as the contract rather than tightening it:
   132	rejecting a mismatched connector on update is a behavior change outside #169's scope, and it belongs to
   133	its own issue.
   134	
   135	**Server code is verified against the spec, not rewritten.** Apart from the reason and userinfo
   136	changes, the tasks read the existing store, API, and connector paths against each requirement and add a
   137	test only where one is missing. The spec is being written after the implementation here, so the
   138	verification step is what makes the two match rather than merely appear to.
   139	
   140	## Risks / Trade-offs
   141	
   142	- **The spec is written from the code it describes, so a bug in the code becomes a bug in the spec.**
   143	  → The requirements were drafted from the API contract and the issue's intent, then checked against
   144	  the code path by path; the review artifact judges them against the issue, not against the diff.
   145	- **A misconfigured `public_url` silently produces unreachable printed links, and a printed label
   146	  cannot be fixed after the fact.** → Syntactic validation catches typos in scheme and host, and the
   147	  table column surfaces the effective value. Reachability is out of scope by design (see Non-Goals).
   148	- **Tightening `base_url` validation can reject a configuration that saves fine today.** → Only a
   149	  `base_url` carrying userinfo, which Homebox auth never needed (the credential travels as a Bearer
   150	  header), and only on the next save of that connection. The proposal calls it out as the change's one
   151	  breaking edge.
   152	- **Two capabilities now describe the same connection record.** → `connections` owns the record and
   153	  its CRUD; `connector-field-transforms` owns the transform rules; the record requirement says so in
   154	  the text, so a later reader is not left choosing.
   155	- **The connection form is now long: six fields plus a transforms editor.** → The field row already
   156	  wraps (`flex flex-wrap`, `ConnectionsSection.tsx:71`), and the public url field takes the same
   157	  `flex-1` sizing as base url, so narrow viewports get another line rather than a squeezed input.
   158	- **Nothing in the product tells an operator that a stored `public_url` on a disabled or unused
   159	  connection is stale.** → Out of scope; the column at least makes it visible.
   160	
   161	## Open Questions
   162	
   163	- Should `PUT /api/connections/{id}` reject a payload whose `connector` differs from the stored one,
   164	  instead of ignoring it? Answering it changes no requirement here (the spec records today's
   165	  behavior), and the change is outside #169. It is a follow-up issue, not a task in this change.
   166	
   167	## Migration Plan
   168	
   169	No data migration: the `public_url` column shipped with the server work and is already present in every
   170	deployed database. No config change, no restart semantics change. Deploying this change makes an
   171	existing column editable; rolling it back hides the field again and leaves stored values in place and
   172	still honoured by the server.

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# connector-field-transforms Specification
     2	
     3	## Purpose
     4	
     5	Defines the connection-scoped rules that derive new label fields from the values a connector returns:
     6	what a rule is, when the service accepts one, where in the read path it is applied, and what a row
     7	carries when a rule does not match. It is the seam between what the upstream returns and what a
     8	template binds to, for every connector.
     9	
    10	## Requirements
    11	
    12	### Requirement: A connection carries an ordered list of field transforms
    13	
    14	A connection SHALL carry a `transforms` list. Each entry is `{ resource, source, pattern }`:
    15	
    16	- `resource` is the id of a resource offered by that connection's connector;
    17	- `source` is the key of a text-valued field of that resource;
    18	- `pattern` is a regular expression whose **named capture groups** name the fields the rule derives.
    19	
    20	The list is ordered and the order is preserved as stored. A connection with an empty list SHALL behave
    21	exactly as a connection with no transforms concept at all: nothing about its schema, browse rows, or
    22	materialized rows changes.
    23	
    24	`POST /api/connections` and `PUT /api/connections/{id}` SHALL accept `transforms`. Omitting it on
    25	`POST` SHALL store an empty list. Omitting it on `PUT` SHALL keep the stored list, matching how an
    26	omitted `credential` keeps the stored credential. Sending an empty list on `PUT` SHALL clear the list.
    27	
    28	`GET /api/connections` and `GET /api/connections/{id}` SHALL return the stored `transforms`. Unlike
    29	`credential`, a transform is not a secret and is returned in full.
    30	
    31	This requirement supersedes the `docs/SPEC.md` §12 sentence "A connection is
    32	`{ id, connector, name, base_url, credential, enabled }` stored in SQLite" and the §12 connection
    33	endpoint table's account of the `POST`/`PUT` bodies, to the extent of adding `transforms`. Everything
    34	else those sections state about connections, including that the credential is never returned, is
    35	unchanged.
    36	
    37	#### Scenario: A connection round-trips its transforms
    38	
    39	- **WHEN** a connection is created with two transforms
    40	- **THEN** the response and every later read of that connection return both, in the order supplied
    41	
    42	#### Scenario: An update that omits transforms keeps them
    43	
    44	- **WHEN** a connection holding transforms is updated with a body that has no `transforms` key
    45	- **THEN** the stored transforms are unchanged
    46	
    47	#### Scenario: An update with an empty list clears them
    48	
    49	- **WHEN** a connection holding transforms is updated with `transforms: []`
    50	- **THEN** the connection holds no transforms
    51	- **AND** its schema, browse rows, and materialized rows carry no derived fields
    52	
    53	#### Scenario: An existing connection predating the feature has none
    54	
    55	- **WHEN** a connection stored before this capability existed is read
    56	- **THEN** its `transforms` is an empty list
    57	- **AND** it browses and materializes exactly as before
    58	
    59	### Requirement: A transform is validated when the connection is saved
    60	
    61	The service SHALL validate every transform at save time and SHALL NOT defer any of these faults to
    62	browse, materialize, or render. A `POST` or `PUT` carrying a rejected transform SHALL fail with
    63	`400 InvalidRequest` and `details.reason` `connection_transform_invalid`, SHALL name the offending
    64	rule by its zero-based index and state the cause in `message`, and SHALL leave the stored connection
    65	untouched.
    66	
    67	A transform SHALL be rejected when any of the following holds:
    68	
    69	- `pattern` is not a valid regular expression, or its compiled form exceeds the compiled-size budget
    70	  of the bounds requirement below;
    71	- `pattern` declares no named capture group;
    72	- `resource` is not a resource id offered by that connection's connector;
    73	- `source` is not a text-valued field of that resource;
    74	- a capture-group name equals a field key the connector already declares for that resource;
    75	- a capture-group name is repeated within the same rule, or is produced by another rule on the same
    76	  resource;
    77	- a capture-group name is `datetime`, or begins with `datetime.` or `vars.`. Those tokens are
    78	  resolved by the renderer before request data is consulted and are excluded from a template's data
    79	  fields, so a derived field carrying such a name could be advertised by the schema and mapped in the
    80	  UI and still never reach a label.
    81	
    82	A connector MAY declare that a resource carries text fields under a key prefix whose names it
    83	discovers from the upstream at runtime; Homebox's per-item custom fields, keyed `custom:<name>`, are
    84	the case that exists. A `source` under such a prefix SHALL be accepted without proving that the
    85	upstream carries that field, because validation does not contact the upstream. The cost is bounded
    86	and deliberate: a rule sourcing a custom field that does not exist is not an error, it simply never
    87	matches, and falls under the non-match requirement below.
    88	
    89	Because a derived name may never equal a field the connector declares, no rule can read another rule's
    90	output: transforms are a single flat pass over what the connector returned, and chaining is
    91	unreachable rather than merely discouraged.
    92	
    93	Validation SHALL NOT contact the upstream system. A connection whose upstream is unreachable, or whose
    94	credential is wrong, SHALL still be able to save and correct its transforms.
    95	
    96	This requirement supersedes the `docs/SPEC.md` §12 sentence "`POST` rejects an unknown `connector`, a
    97	missing `credential`, or an invalid `base_url` with `400`" by adding transform validation to it; the
    98	three faults it names are unchanged.
    99	
   100	#### Scenario: A pattern that does not compile is refused
   101	
   102	- **WHEN** a connection is saved with a transform whose `pattern` is not a valid regular expression
   103	- **THEN** the response is `400` with `details.reason` `connection_transform_invalid`
   104	- **AND** the message names the rule's index
   105	- **AND** nothing is stored
   106	
   107	#### Scenario: A pattern with no named group is refused
   108	
   109	- **WHEN** a transform's pattern matches but declares only unnamed groups
   110	- **THEN** the save is refused, because the rule can name no field
   111	
   112	#### Scenario: A derived name that shadows an upstream field is refused
   113	
   114	- **WHEN** a transform on a resource that already declares a `name` field derives a group called `name`
   115	- **THEN** the save is refused

   763	fn row_to_connection(r: &rusqlite::Row<'_>) -> rusqlite::Result<Connection> {
   764	    let raw_transforms: Option<String> = r.get(7)?;
   765	    let transforms = match raw_transforms {
   766	        Some(s) if !s.trim().is_empty() => serde_json::from_str(&s).map_err(|e| {
   767	            rusqlite::Error::FromSqlConversionFailure(7, rusqlite::types::Type::Text, Box::new(e))
   768	        })?,
   769	        _ => Vec::new(),
   770	    };
   771	    Ok(Connection {
   772	        id: r.get(0)?,
   773	        connector: r.get(1)?,
   774	        name: r.get(2)?,
   775	        base_url: r.get(3)?,
   776	        credential: r.get(4)?,
   777	        enabled: r.get::<_, i64>(5)? != 0,
   778	        public_url: r.get(6)?,
   779	        transforms,
   780	    })
   781	}
