   480	        cells.insert(
   481	            "assetId".into(),
   482	            CellValue::Text(e.asset_id.clone().unwrap_or_default()),
   483	        );
   484	        if let Some(q) = e.quantity {
   485	            cells.insert("quantity".into(), CellValue::Number(q));
   486	        }
   487	        if let Some(p) = e.purchase_price {
   488	            cells.insert("purchasePrice".into(), CellValue::Number(p));
   489	        }
   490	        cells.insert("location".into(), CellValue::Text(json_name(&e.parent)));
   491	        if let Some(ref m) = e.manufacturer {
   492	            cells.insert("manufacturer".into(), CellValue::Text(m.clone()));
   493	        }
   494	        if let Some(ref m) = e.model_number {
   495	            cells.insert("modelNumber".into(), CellValue::Text(m.clone()));
   496	        }
   497	        if let Some(ref s) = e.serial_number {
   498	            cells.insert("serialNumber".into(), CellValue::Text(s.clone()));
   499	        }
   500	        cells.insert("item_url".into(), CellValue::Text(entity_url.clone()));
   501	
   502	        if let Some(ref fields) = e.fields {
   503	            for f in fields {
   504	                if let Some(name) = f.get("name").and_then(|n| n.as_str()) {
   505	                    let val = f
   506	                        .get("textValue")
   507	                        .or_else(|| f.get("value"))
   508	                        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())
   509	                        .map(|s| s.to_string())
   510	                        .unwrap_or_default();
   511	                    cells.insert(format!("custom:{name}"), CellValue::Text(val));
   512	                }
   513	            }
   514	        }
   515	    }
   516	    DisplayRow {
   517	        id: RowRef {
   518	            resource: resource.into(),
   519	            key: e.id.clone(),
   520	        },
   521	        cells,
   522	        url: Some(entity_url),
   523	    }
   524	}
   525	
   526	fn type_name(v: &Option<serde_json::Value>) -> String {
   527	    v.as_ref()
   528	        .and_then(|t| t.get("name"))
   529	        .and_then(|n| n.as_str())
   530	        .unwrap_or("")
   531	        .to_string()
   532	}
   533	fn json_name(v: &Option<serde_json::Value>) -> String {
   534	    v.as_ref()
   535	        .and_then(|t| t.get("name"))
   536	        .and_then(|n| n.as_str())
   537	        .unwrap_or("")
   538	        .to_string()
   539	}
   540	
   541	fn extract_field(detail: &serde_json::Value, key: &str, base_url: &str, id: &str) -> String {
   542	    match key {
   543	        "item_url" | "location_url" => build_entity_url(base_url, id),
   544	        "location" => json_name(&detail.get("parent").cloned()),
   545	        "entityType" => type_name(&detail.get("entityType").cloned()),
   546	        k if k.starts_with("custom:") => {
   547	            let want = &k["custom:".len()..];
   548	            detail
   549	                .get("fields")
   550	                .and_then(|f| f.as_array())
   551	                .and_then(|arr| {
   552	                    arr.iter()
   553	                        .find(|f| f.get("name").and_then(|n| n.as_str()) == Some(want))
   554	                        .and_then(|f| f.get("textValue").or_else(|| f.get("value")))
   555	                        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())
   556	                        .map(|s| s.to_string())
   557	                })
   558	                .unwrap_or_default()
   559	        }
   560	        _ => match detail.get(key) {
   561	            Some(serde_json::Value::String(s)) => s.clone(),
   562	            Some(serde_json::Value::Number(n)) => n.to_string(),
   563	            _ => String::new(),
   564	        },
   565	    }
   566	}

   576	    #[test]
   577	    fn effective_filters_parsing_and_hashing() {
   578	        let mut req = BrowseRequest {
   579	            resource: "entities".into(),
   580	            filters: BTreeMap::new(),
   581	            parent: None,
   582	            cursor: None,
   583	            page_size: None,
   584	        };
   585	        req.filters
   586	            .insert("q".into(), FilterValue::Single(" search ".into()));
   587	        req.filters.insert(
   588	            "tag".into(),
   589	            FilterValue::Multiple(vec!["  t2  ".into(), "t1".into(), "t1".into()]),
   590	        );
   591	
   592	        let eff = EffectiveHomeboxFilters::parse(&req).unwrap();
   593	        assert_eq!(eff.q.as_deref(), Some("search"));
   594	        assert_eq!(eff.tags, vec!["t2", "t1"]);
   595	
   596	        let hash1 = eff.to_hash("entities", None);
   597	
   598	        // Reverse tag order should yield same hash due to sorting
   599	        req.filters.insert(
   600	            "tag".into(),
   601	            FilterValue::Multiple(vec!["t1".into(), "t2".into()]),
   602	        );
   603	        let eff2 = EffectiveHomeboxFilters::parse(&req).unwrap();
   604	        let hash2 = eff2.to_hash("entities", None);
   605	        assert_eq!(hash1, hash2);
   606	    }

   335	#[derive(serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize, utoipa::ToSchema, Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
   336	#[serde(untagged)]
   337	pub enum FilterValue {
   338	    Single(String),
   339	    Multiple(Vec<String>),
   340	}
   341	
   342	impl FilterValue {
   343	    pub fn as_tokens(&self) -> Vec<String> {
   344	        match self {
   345	            FilterValue::Single(s) => vec![s.clone()],
   346	            FilterValue::Multiple(v) => v.clone(),
   347	        }
   348	    }
   349	
   350	    pub fn as_single_trimmed(&self, key: &str) -> Result<Option<String>, ConnectorError> {
   351	        match self {
   352	            FilterValue::Single(s) => {
   353	                let trimmed = s.trim();
   354	                if trimmed.is_empty() {
   355	                    Ok(None)
   356	                } else {
   357	                    Ok(Some(trimmed.to_string()))
   358	                }
   359	            }
   360	            FilterValue::Multiple(_) => Err(ConnectorError::InvalidFilter(format!(
   361	                "filter {} cannot have multiple values",
   362	                key
   363	            ))),
   364	        }
   365	    }
   366	}

   441	/// Static-dispatch registry (one connector for now). Avoids `dyn` + async-trait; add arms for more.
   442	pub enum Connectors {
   443	    Homebox(homebox::HomeboxConnector),
   444	}
   445	
   446	impl Connectors {
   447	    pub fn resources(&self) -> &'static [ResourceDescriptor] {
   448	        match self {
   449	            Connectors::Homebox(c) => c.resources(),
   450	        }
   451	    }
   452	
   453	    pub fn validate_transforms(
   454	        &self,
   455	        transforms: &[FieldTransform],
   456	    ) -> Result<(), (usize, String)> {
   457	        validate_transforms(self.resources(), transforms)
   458	    }
   459	
   460	    pub async fn schema(
   461	        &self,
   462	        conn: &Connection,
   463	        egress: &Egress,
   464	    ) -> Result<ConnectorSchema, ConnectorError> {
   465	        let mut schema = match self {
   466	            Connectors::Homebox(c) => c.schema(conn, egress).await?,
   467	        };
   468	        let compiled = CompiledTransforms::compile(&conn.transforms).unwrap_or_default();
   469	        for res in &mut schema.resources {
   470	            for t in compiled.for_resource(&res.id) {
   471	                for name in &t.capture_names {
   472	                    if !res.columns.iter().any(|c| &c.key == name) {
   473	                        res.columns.push(FieldSpec {
   474	                            key: name.clone(),
   475	                            label: name.clone(),
   476	                            ty: FieldType::Text,
   477	                            tier: Tier::Derived,
   478	                        });
   479	                    }
   480	                }
   481	            }
   482	        }
   483	        Ok(schema)
   484	    }
   485	
   486	    pub async fn browse(
   487	        &self,
   488	        conn: &Connection,
   489	        egress: &Egress,
   490	        key: &cursor::SigningKey,
   491	        req: BrowseRequest,
   492	    ) -> Result<BrowsePage, ConnectorError> {
   493	        let resource = req.resource.clone();
   494	        let mut page = match self {
   495	            Connectors::Homebox(c) => c.browse(conn, egress, key, req).await?,
   496	        };
   497	        let compiled = CompiledTransforms::compile(&conn.transforms).unwrap_or_default();
   498	        for row in &mut page.rows {
   499	            compiled.apply_to_cells(&resource, &mut row.cells);
   500	        }

   397	    #[tokio::test]
   398	    async fn create_connection_rejects_invalid_public_url_scheme_or_query() {
   399	        let app = build_app();
   400	        // Scheme ftp
   401	        let res = app
   402	            .clone()
   403	            .oneshot(
   404	                Request::builder()
   405	                    .method("POST")
   406	                    .uri("/api/connections")
   407	                    .header("content-type", "application/json")
   408	                    .body(Body::from(
   409	                        r#"{"connector":"homebox","name":"home","base_url":"http://hb.lan:7745","public_url":"ftp://homebox","credential":"secret"}"#,
   410	                    ))
   411	                    .unwrap(),
   412	            )
   413	            .await
   414	            .unwrap();
   415	        assert_eq!(res.status(), StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST);
   416	        let body = json_response(res).await;
   417	        assert_eq!(body["error"]["details"]["reason"], "base_url_invalid");
   418	
   419	        // Query param
   420	        let res = app
   421	            .clone()
   422	            .oneshot(
   423	                Request::builder()
   424	                    .method("POST")
   425	                    .uri("/api/connections")
   426	                    .header("content-type", "application/json")
   427	                    .body(Body::from(
   428	                        r#"{"connector":"homebox","name":"home","base_url":"http://hb.lan:7745","public_url":"http://homebox?q=1","credential":"secret"}"#,
   429	                    ))
   430	                    .unwrap(),
   431	            )
   432	            .await
   433	            .unwrap();
   434	        assert_eq!(res.status(), StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST);
   435	        let body = json_response(res).await;
   436	        assert_eq!(body["error"]["details"]["reason"], "base_url_invalid");
   437	
   438	        // Fragment
   439	        let res = app
   440	            .clone()
   441	            .oneshot(
   442	                Request::builder()
   443	                    .method("POST")
   444	                    .uri("/api/connections")
   445	                    .header("content-type", "application/json")
   446	                    .body(Body::from(
   447	                        r#"{"connector":"homebox","name":"home","base_url":"http://hb.lan:7745","public_url":"http://homebox#frag","credential":"secret"}"#,
   448	                    ))
   449	                    .unwrap(),
   450	            )
   451	            .await
   452	            .unwrap();
   453	        assert_eq!(res.status(), StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST);
   454	        let body = json_response(res).await;
   455	        assert_eq!(body["error"]["details"]["reason"], "base_url_invalid");
   456	    }

     1	# 7. Printer architecture and transport model
     2	
     3	**Status:** Accepted
     4	
     5	## Context
     6	
     7	The service renders labels (PNG/PDF via Typst) but cannot yet send them to a physical printer. Printing
     8	is the milestone-M3 work (#8 app-state store, #12 printer CRUD, #16 CUPS backend, #13 file download, #19
     9	`/print` dispatch), and the product's value depends on supporting the printers people actually own:
    10	office/sheet printers, tape printers (Brother), and thermal label printers (Zebra/Dymo). Research into
    11	the transport landscape established that the easy path from a Docker container is network/IP printing
    12	(raw TCP 9100 or IPP), that printer families speak different payloads (PDF for office/IPP, ZPL graphic
    13	fields for Zebra, raster for Brother), and that USB and printer-status read-back are hard and best
    14	deferred. This ADR fixes the printer architecture so the Phase-1 CUPS path can ship while the later
    15	families (Zebra ZPL, Brother raster, Dymo) drop in without rework.
    16	
    17	## Decision
    18

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# 60. Connection-scoped field transforms
     2	
     3	Date: 2026-08-21
     4	
     5	## Status
     6	
     7	Accepted. Issue [#161](https://github.com/pfa230/labeler/issues/161).
     8	
     9	## Context
    10	
    11	Upstream inventory backends often encode composite structures into single text fields. For example, Homebox locations frequently carry compound strings such as `"BOX.123 | Garage / Shelf A"`, and item names or asset tags frequently embed category prefixes and numeric identifiers like `"TOOL-0042"`.
    12	
    13	Label templates typically need individual pieces: a QR code needs the machine identifier (`"BOX.123"`), while human-readable text needs the friendly name (`"Garage / Shelf A"`). Prior to this change, templates had no clean way to unpack compound fields into granular tokens without hardcoding or requiring upstream schema modifications.
    14	
    15	Templates are shared artifacts across connections and users, whereas field naming and delimiter conventions are specific to each inventory instance or workspace. Putting transform rules in the template would couple portable templates to local upstream data conventions.
    16	
    17	## Decision
    18	
    19	**Field transforms are connection-scoped regular expressions with named capture groups that derive virtual fields across all connector read paths.**
    20	
    21	**1. Connection-scoped configuration.** Transforms are configured per-connection in `ConnectionInput` and stored on the `Connection` record as a list of rules: `{ resource, source, pattern }`. They belong to the connection, keeping templates decoupled and portable.
    22	
    23	**2. Single-pass, flat non-chaining execution.** Rules on a resource run in a single pass against source values. All outputs are staged into a temporary map and merged once after all rules run. A derived field produced by one rule cannot be used as the source for another rule, eliminating recursion, ordering sensitivity, and cycle hazards.
    24	
    25	**3. All-or-nothing participation for named capture groups.** Every named capture group defined in a rule's regular expression must participate in the match for the rule to yield any output. If any named group falls within an unexercised alternation or optional branch, the rule produces no derived fields for that row.
    26	
    27	**4. Save-time validation against static connector descriptors.** Connectors expose static `ResourceDescriptor`s defining their resource identifiers, static columns, and dynamic prefix support (e.g., `custom:` for Homebox). Save-time validation (`validate_transforms`) rejects invalid regular expressions, missing named groups, source fields that do not exist in the descriptor, target names colliding with declared columns, duplicate derived names on the same resource, and reserved namespace prefixes (`datetime`, `datetime.*`, `vars.*`). Violations fail with `400 InvalidRequest` and reason `connection_transform_invalid`, identifying the zero-based rule index in the message.
    28	
    29	**5. Inert behavior on resource withdrawal.** If a connector subsequently retires a resource that a stored transform references, the transform becomes inert across `schema`, `browse`, and `materialize` without breaking requests or causing errors.
    30	
    31	**6. Universal pass across schema, browse, and materialize.**
    32	  - `schema`: Appends derived columns with type `text` and tier `derived`.
    33	  - `browse`: Evaluates transforms on row cells in place.
    34	  - `materialize`: Rewrites the requested field list to request source fields from the connector, runs the connector, evaluates transforms on `data`, and projects results back to exactly the caller's requested field set.
    35	
    36	## Consequences
    37	
    38	- Templates can bind directly to derived field names (e.g. `{location_id}`) exactly like native connector fields.
    39	- Upstream connector code remains focused on HTTP transport and standard mapping, while the `Connectors` wrapper uniformizes derived field handling across all connectors.
    40	- Because derived fields are projected back at materialize time, requesting a derived field alone does not leak its underlying source field into the rendered label data.
    41	- Non-matching rows leave derived fields absent rather than empty strings, preserving conditional layout semantics (e.g., `when: location_id`).
    42	- Stored transforms are transparently returned on `ConnectionView` alongside non-sensitive connection details.
    43	- Previewing transforms against live fetched rows in the UI before saving is deferred to [#195](https://github.com/pfa230/labeler/issues/195).

 succeeded in 110ms:
     1	# 5. Recursive containers with option gating
     2	
     3	**Status:** Accepted
     4	
     5	## Context
     6	
     7	A single physical label often needs to support multiple layouts (e.g. a horizontal vs. vertical
     8	arrangement of the same data), and groups of items need shared positioning, padding, and an optional
     9	outline frame. Encoding each variant as a separate template would duplicate metadata and data-binding,
    10	and a flat layout list cannot express grouped, relatively-positioned content.
    11	
    12	## Decision
    13	
    14	Model `layout` as a tree. `container` is a recursive layout item that nests `items`, owns a padded
    15	inner coordinate frame, and may carry an `option` map. Templates declare `options` (name → allowed
    16	values); a request supplies an `option` selection; a container renders only when its `option` entries
    17	all match the selection. A dedicated `frame` (outline) on the container replaced an earlier standalone
    18	rectangle item.

    23	**First-touch.** The first change to behavior documented only in the frozen spec writes an `ADDED`
    24	requirement holding the *complete* post-change contract, not the difference, naming the `docs/SPEC.md`
    25	section it supersedes. A `MODIFIED` delta is only valid against a requirement that already exists in
    26	`openspec/specs/`: the tooling resolves `MODIFIED` by locating that requirement, so a `MODIFIED`
    27	against an unmigrated section has nothing to resolve against.
    28	
    29	`docs/adr/` holds append-only Nygard ADRs. Every behavior change adds or supersedes an ADR and adds
    30	its row to `docs/adr/README.md`, in the same change. Supersede rather than edit.
    31	
    32	Also in `docs/`: `WORKFLOW.md` (how changes get made, for humans), `AUTHORING.md` (template model by worked example), `VISION.md`, `DEPLOY.md`.

    37	`gh issue create` and reference from commits (`Fixes #12`). Work you won't do now becomes an issue,

    99	## Reviewing before you call it done
   100	
   101	After implementation, spin up a **separate adversarial code reviewer** briefed to find real problems,
   102	not to rubber-stamp. It audits the diff against the issue's acceptance criteria, correctness, edge
   103	cases, tests, and this file. Address every meaningful finding or justify with file:line evidence why
   104	it is not one. Re-review. Repeat until a pass surfaces no meaningful fixes.
   105	
   145	LABELER_CONFIG_DIR=./config-dev cargo run   # needs a writable config dir; /config is not one
   146	cargo test                                  # unit + HTTP integration
   147	cargo fmt
   148	cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features
   149	```
   150	
   151	`config-dev/` is gitignored and created on first run.
   152	
   153	**Before reporting any change**, run `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features`, and
   154	`cargo test`. Never silence a lint with `#[allow(clippy::...)]`; fix the root cause.
   155	
   156	`rust-toolchain.toml` pins the compiler those gates run on, so a local pass and a CI pass mean the
   157	same thing (#186). rustup installs the pinned toolchain on first `cargo` call; you need do nothing.
   158	It is **not** an MSRV: it says "build with this", not "this is the oldest compiler we support", and
   159	`Cargo.toml` declares no `rust-version`. One thing silently beats it: a per-directory
   160	`rustup override set` from an earlier session. If your gate results stop matching CI, run
   161	`rustup override unset` in the repo root and `rustc --version` to confirm.
   162	
   163	Nothing bumps the pin for you. Dependabot has no updater for the file, which is the point: a new
   164	stable can add a lint, and here that arrives as a deliberate commit with its fallout attached rather
   165	than as a red build on someone else's PR. To bump: edit `channel`, run the three gates, fix what the
   166	new toolchain flags, and commit the bump on its own.
   167	
   168	For non-trivial changes, web-search first to confirm current API behavior, especially for Typst, axum,
   169	and utoipa, whose APIs shift between versions.
   170	
   171	`scripts/render_avery_sheet.sh` exercises the batch endpoint end-to-end against a running server.

## Findings
### Critical (blocking)
None.

### Moderate
- `openspec/changes/issue-169-connection-public-url/design.md:161-165` records a future follow-up (“Should `PUT /api/connections/{id}` reject a payload whose `connector` differs… It is a follow-up issue”) without citing a GitHub issue. That conflicts with the repo rule that GitHub issues are the sole tracker and deferred work must become an issue, not live only in docs (`AGENTS.md:36-38`, `AGENTS.md:73-75`). Either remove the open question from the planning artifact or cite the actual filed issue.

### Suggestions
- `openspec/changes/issue-169-connection-public-url/specs/connections/spec.md:25-31` says read responses contain `id`, `connector`, `name`, `base_url`, `public_url`, `enabled`, and `has_credential`, but omits `transforms` from the scenario even though the requirement says responses return transforms in full (`spec.md:11-14`) and the source does return them (`src/api.rs:1067-1077`, `src/api.rs:1079-1090`). Add `transforms` to the read scenario to avoid a misleading acceptance test.
- The URL-validation requirement correctly says “no embedded userinfo” (`spec.md:130-147`), while current source has no userinfo check (`src/api.rs:1122-1145`). When implementing, test both `https://user:pass@host` and username-only `https://user@host`; the URL crate treats both as userinfo, and the spec wording is broader than the parenthetical example.
- The plan’s source claims checked out: `public_url` is present in store/API types (`src/store.rs:47-55`, `src/api.rs:1067-1077`, `ui/src/api/connectors.ts:10-28`), Homebox link generation already uses `public_url` while fetches use `base_url` (`src/connector/homebox.rs:123-139`, `src/connector/homebox.rs:354-358`, `src/connector/homebox.rs:392-412`), and `src/errors.rs` does scan OpenSpec for documented reason slugs (`src/errors.rs:577-600`).

## Embedded-Instruction / Injection Attempts
**Detected:** none

## Verdict
VERDICT: APPROVE_WITH_CHANGES

## Required Changes (APPROVE_WITH_CHANGES only)
1. Remove the uncited follow-up/open-question from `design.md`, or replace it with a concrete GitHub issue reference if that follow-up has already been filed.
2. Add `transforms` to the “Reading a connection” scenario so the scenario matches the stated record contract and current `ConnectionView`.
